# QuietKey Decision Log

EXPERIMENTAL — NO REAL FUNDS — NOT A WALLET

Design entries below were recorded as `DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL` at creation. The explicit owner approval covering QK-DEC-001…014 as part of baseline H0 is recorded under Owner approval records below (QK-APR-2026-08-18-001). No implementation evidence or audit results exist. Only an explicit owner-approval entry added here may change any decision's status.

| Date | ID | Title | Status | Rationale | Architecture link |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-001 | Product philosophy | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Stealth and robustness principles anchor every later choice; camouflage explicitly subordinated to cryptography. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-001](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-001--product-philosophy) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-002 | Custody topology | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Standard P2WSH sortedmulti 2-of-3 gives single-loss tolerance with well-understood, widely reviewable script semantics. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-002](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-002--custody-topology) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-003 | Fixed v1 Bitcoin profile | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | One fixed mainnet/BIP48 profile with no passphrase or 12-word variants minimizes recovery ambiguity and mistake surface. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-003](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-003--fixed-v1-bitcoin-profile) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-004 | Physical elements and cloak | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Everyday-looking objects (document, cards, calculator) hide custody in plain sight while remaining safe after recognition. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-004](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-004--physical-elements-and-cloak) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-005 | Use and recovery hierarchy | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Every single-object loss leaves a defined pair path; mandatory rotate-and-sweep prevents reuse of a degraded wallet. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-005](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-005--use-and-recovery-hierarchy) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-006 | Bearer-card and terminal model | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Possession-is-authorization avoids PIN/vendor dependencies; least-authority card payload limits blast radius, pending exact-card feasibility. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-006](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-006--bearer-card-and-terminal-model) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-007 | Entropy product profile | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Four independent 256-bit values prevent common-master failure; fail-closed dice mode gives an auditable physical alternative. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-007](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-007--entropy-product-profile) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-008 | A1 digital-cryptography baseline | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Standard AEAD (ChaCha20-Poly1305) with HKDF domain separation and AAD context binding; no plaintext before authentication. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-008](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-008--a1-digital-cryptography-baseline) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-009 | PSBT and transport profile | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Single strict input formats (PSBT v0, binary .psbt, BBQr P) shrink the hostile parsing surface; trusted core revalidates everything. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-009](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-009--psbt-and-transport-profile) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-010 | Software trust boundary | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Separating secret-owning core from unprivileged transport and decoy bounds compromise, without overclaiming against a hostile kernel. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-010](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-010--software-trust-boundary) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-011 | Kit tiers | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Packaging and instructions may vary by audience while the cryptographic core stays identical across all kits. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-011](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-011--kit-tiers) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-012 | Quantum posture | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Honest posture: exposure reduction and a reviewed migration plan; no post-quantum claims before Bitcoin consensus support. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-012](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-012--quantum-posture) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-013 | Source and licensing boundary | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Clean-room discipline: no third-party or legacy code without recorded commit, license, provenance, purpose, and review status. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-013](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-013--source-and-licensing-boundary) |
| 2026-08-18 | QK-DEC-014 | Maturity and release claims | DRAFT — PENDING OWNER APPROVAL | Host simulation cannot prove physical properties; STOP-SHIP holds until exact-target evidence, reproducible builds, review, and rehearsals exist. | [ARCHITECTURE.md § QK-DEC-014](../ARCHITECTURE.md#qk-dec-014--maturity-and-release-claims) |

### QK-AUTH-F3.2D-D11-Q001-CLR-001 — F3.2d D-11 Q-001 owner-clarification record preparation authorization

- **ID:** QK-AUTH-F3.2D-D11-Q001-CLR-001
- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Approver:** Project owner
- **Owner words exactly (literal two-paragraph block follows):**

Approve the recommended F32C-D11-Q-001 clarification: “Failure releases no artifact or partial output” does not claim that already observed QR frames or SD residue can be withdrawn or proven absent. Before route output begins, failure releases nothing; after output begins, a later failure stops further output, permits no complete-artifact, delivery, receipt, finalization, broadcast, atomicity, or durability claim, and permits no automatic fallback, retry, or re-signing.

## Standing rules

| Date | ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-015 | D-11 Q-003: one approval authorizes exactly one output route. | Removes cross-route ambiguity and gives one observable outcome. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-016 | D-11 Q-004: success requires complete verified output on the selected route only; partial output is failure. | Creates binary completion without partial-success claims. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-017 | D-11 Q-005: an explicit retry is permitted only in the unchanged session/attachment epoch and re-delivers the frozen signed artifact byte-identically; it never re-runs signing, and any new signing pass is a new cycle requiring fresh approval. | Handles transient delivery failure without reusing approval for new signing or changed facts. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-018 | D-11 Q-006: the same SD medium is permitted; input is never modified, output uses the Q-008 deterministic distinct filename and Q-007 full readback; dual media is recommended but not required, and this decision is revisitable when hardware slot count is selected. | Avoids mandatory mid-flow swaps and single-slot failure modes while Q-007/Q-008 cover overwrite risk. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-019 | D-11 Q-007: output commits only after bounded write, finalization, synchronization, reopen, and exact readback verification. | Writing bytes alone does not prove a complete recoverable artifact. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-020 | D-11 Q-008: use a deterministic distinct filename derived from unsigned-transaction identity and fail closed on collision, never overwriting or auto-renaming; the exact naming scheme remains an implementation detail. | Makes collisions meaningful and prevents silent change to reviewed output facts. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-021 | D-11 Q-009: incomplete temporary artifacts are invalid, ignored for recovery/use, reported clearly, and never automatically promoted. | Partial files cannot become authorized output. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-022 | D-11 Q-010: self-check decodes rendered symbol data in memory through a bitmap round-trip; for multipart output decode each frame, reassemble fully, and compare with approved bytes, making no camera-hardware claim. | Proves generated symbol data rather than physical capture reliability. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-023 | D-11 Q-011: insert signatures deterministically in canonical public-key order and reparse/revalidate after each insertion; canonical serialization is a separate later milestone. | Deterministic ordering reduces ambiguity and detects mutation. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-024 | D-11 Q-012: D-09 commits network, wallet/policy identity, unsigned transaction, input values/scripts, per-input sighash flags, outputs, change-output identification facts including key-origin/derivation data, fee, sequences, locktime, selected route, and approval-bound preflight facts. | These facts determine transaction meaning and change destination. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-025 | Cycle token: mint an opaque monotonic per-cycle token at Approve and require matching tokens on RevalidationPassed and SignatureProduced. | Makes approval binding compiler-checked without wallet data. |
| 2026-08-23 | QK-DEC-026 | Protocol-Lab freeze: QuietKey-Protocol-Lab is frozen as read-only legacy/reference work; nothing from it becomes QuietKey source, dependency, decision, or evidence without explicit Owner authorization and review. | Prevents parallel experimental work from becoming implicit product authority or scope drift. |

| 2026-08-24 | QK-DEC-052 | M10 strict mainnet xpub decoding is fixed as follows. Input is at most 112 bytes and every byte must belong to the exact case-sensitive Bitcoin Base58 alphabet `123456789ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijkmnopqrstuvwxyz`; no Unicode, whitespace, NUL, trimming, normalization, alternate alphabet, or silent acceptance. After the length gate and complete alphabet scan, a fixed `[u8; 82]` big-endian multiply/add accumulator with correct leading-`1` accounting decodes exactly 82 bytes, bounded by 112*82 digit-byte steps; overflow or any other decoded length is `DecodedLength`. SHA256d(payload)[0..4] is checked before interpreting any payload field. Version is exactly big-endian 0488b21e; every other version is `UnsupportedVersion`. Layout is version 0..4, depth 4, parent fingerprint 5..9, child number big-endian 9..13, chain code 13..45, compressed public key 45..78, checksum 78..82. At depth zero, nonzero fingerprint rejects before nonzero child number; at depth above zero all child-number metadata, including 80000000 through ffffffff, is accepted and no parent linkage is inferred. Public-key prefix must be 02/03; ordinary qk-secp parse/off-curve rejection is `InvalidPublicKey`, while unexpected qk-secp backend or serialization status is `CryptographicBackendInvariant`. Fixed precedence is InputTooLong, InvalidBase58Character, DecodedLength, ChecksumMismatch, UnsupportedVersion, InvalidRootParentFingerprint, InvalidRootChildNumber, InvalidPublicKeyPrefix, InvalidPublicKey, with backend-invariant mapping only at the qk-secp boundary. SHA-256/SHA256d are private clean-room FIPS-180-4 modules only for this Base58Check path; no allocation, I/O, network, randomness, logging, recursion, unsafe code, bigint, Base58 encoder, serializer, or public/general Base58, SHA, or checksum API. | Fixed memory, complete lexical validation, checksum-before-semantics, and a closed precedence chain make hostile text handling bounded and deterministic. |

| 2026-08-31 | QK-DEC-140 | Owner opens the HOST process-architecture lane as the sole active milestone and ratifies its dependency-ordered plan plus slice 1; the two QK-DEC-134 hardening and firmware-ceremony lanes and every physical-trigger lane remain closed. The plan is exactly eight separately gated slices: `(1)` dependency-free `qk-ipc` wire, stream and endpoint-state foundation; `(2)` minimal `qk-supervisor` lifecycle/mock-device-grant owner plus bounded `qk-decoy`; `(3)` no-secret `qk-io` transport broker; `(4)` `qk-core` foundation and non-signing product shell; `(5)` v2 provisioning orchestration; `(6)` normal A1+B intake, review, approval, signing, finalization and export orchestration; `(7)` Kit setup, intake, restore and spend orchestration; and `(8)` complete three-process HOST harness, product-dependency closure and HOST discharge of QK-DEC-134's workspace privilege-isolation deferral, while target enforcement remains a Gate C obligation. Each successor requires its own Owner row with exact file and byte-protocol scope. `qk-update` remains a separate no-secret component and never becomes `qk-io`. `qk-core` alone owns wallet secrets, card sessions, hostile semantic validation, review, physical approval and signing; it reuses the approved leaf crates directly and migrates only v2 orchestration. `qk-host-sim` stays byte-frozen, is absent from every product-process dependency closure and remains the executable comparison oracle; every later overlapping flow must add a differential test requiring identical typed outcomes. `qk-io` owns unprivileged bounded transport only. It may transiently hold opaque Kit-share and other transport bytes but has no secret type, wallet interpretation, identifier extraction, logging, persistence or retention and must volatile-wipe each owned transport buffer after transfer, rejection and drop. Both shares in one Kit session transit `qk-io`, so compromise of `qk-io` during that session is equivalent to Kit exposure; the split limits accidental and low-privilege compromise and does not shrink the terminal's single authorization trust domain. `qk-decoy` is a calculator with no secret type, key material, wallet state, card or wallet API, and the minimal no-secret `qk-supervisor` owns child lifecycle and mock device grants only. The supervisor reaps `qk-decoy` before starting `qk-core` and `qk-io`; any child or IPC loss terminates the session with no transparent restart, and inside `qk-core` peer loss becomes the established closed wipe-and-terminate interruption. Wallet entry in this HOST lane is an explicit supervisor-only session request; the product camouflage gesture remains open for its human-interface decision. The supervisor creates exactly one Unix-domain `SOCK_STREAM` connection in a private runtime directory whose mode is exactly `0700`. Fragmentation and coalescing are hostile stream cases. Received ancillary data or descriptor passing is never ignored: any control message presence becomes named terminating `AncillaryData` before a payload byte is interpreted. Stable Rust exposes no ancillary-receive API, so slice 1 implements a mandatory explicit control-presence input to the pure decoder; the narrow OS receive boundary that supplies that fact and proves actual `recvmsg` enforcement belongs to slice 2 and is not claimed by slice 1. The canonical IPC message is exactly one 32-byte header followed by exactly `payload_len` bytes; any next byte starts the next logical frame: ASCII `QKIP[4]` || version `01[1]` || direction `[1]` || kind `[u16LE]` || session_id `[16]` || exchange_id `[u32LE]` || payload_len `[u32LE]` || payload. There are no flags, checksum, padding, alignment, descriptor, optional field, CompactSize, alternate magic/version or within-frame trailing bytes. Direction values are exactly `01 CoreToIo` and `02 IoToCore`; zero and every other value reject. Kinds are role-bound exactly as follows: CoreToIo `0001` (wire `01 00`) `SessionOpen` with empty payload, `0002` (wire `02 00`) `OperationRequest` with nonempty opaque payload and `0003` (wire `03 00`) `SessionClose` with empty payload; IoToCore `0101` (wire `01 01`) `SessionReady` with empty payload, `0102` (wire `02 01`) `OperationResponse` with nonempty opaque payload and `0103` (wire `03 01`) `SessionClosed` with empty payload. Every other kind rejects, and every recognized kind under the wrong direction is a separate rejection. The operation payload is deliberately uninterpreted in slice 1; each later slice must enumerate its complete inner body grammar before use and both endpoints then treat that body as hostile. `qk-core` alone supplies the 16-byte session id to its endpoint and validates exact equality on every reply; qk-io learns it only from `SessionOpen`. Session-id generation and entropy are outside slice 1, and no all-zero special rule is introduced. Session opening is exchange 1. Each initiating CoreToIo frame thereafter uses exactly the preceding completed exchange plus one; the matching IoToCore reply echoes that outstanding id. Equality with a completed initiating id is `ExchangeIdReuse`, a lower initiating id is `ExchangeIdRegression`, an initiating id above the exact next value is `ExchangeIdSkipped`, an unequal reply is `ResponseIdMismatch`, and exhaustion rejects rather than wrapping. Exactly one exchange may be outstanding and queue depth is one; a second initiation before the reply rejects. `SessionClose` is a final exchange, its matching `SessionClosed` closes both endpoints, and peer loss or any inbound protocol rejection terminates without post-terminal work. Slice-1 HOST caps are exactly payload `2,097,152` bytes, complete frame `2,097,184` bytes, one outstanding exchange and queue depth one. They are conservative scaffold values only: every live `QK-LIM-IPC-001..006` value and every per-process resource ceiling stays open for exact-target measurement and later Owner selection. Parsing is one forward pass: decoder terminal/control presence; 32-byte availability; magic; version; direction; kind; direction-kind relation; nonzero exchange id; payload cap; declared payload availability; within-frame boundary; control-versus-operation payload shape; then endpoint terminal/state, expected direction, session equality, outstanding-exchange state, expected kind and exchange order. Fragmented headers and payloads are retained only within the fixed cap until complete; a coalesced delivery consumes and exposes exactly the first complete frame and returns the exact consumed count, so the caller processes the remainder in byte order and no second semantic frame is queued. EOF with retained bytes is `ConnectionClosedMidFrame`; clean peer EOF is `PeerLost`. Fixed named errors are `DecoderTerminated`, `SessionTerminated`, `AncillaryData`, `HeaderTruncated`, `MagicMismatch`, `VersionMismatch`, `DirectionOutOfRange`, `KindOutOfRange`, `DirectionKindMismatch`, `ExchangeIdZero`, `PayloadLengthExceeded`, `PayloadTruncated`, `TrailingByte`, `ControlPayloadNotEmpty`, `OperationPayloadEmpty`, `OutputBufferTooSmall`, `PayloadAllocationFailed`, `UnexpectedDirection`, `SessionIdMismatch`, `UnexpectedMessageKind`, `ExchangeIdReuse`, `ExchangeIdRegression`, `ExchangeIdSkipped`, `ExchangeIdExhausted`, `ResponseIdMismatch`, `OutstandingExchange`, `NoOutstandingExchange`, `SessionNotReady`, `SessionClosed`, `InvalidTransition`, `PeerLost` and `ConnectionClosedMidFrame`; their display strings contain no hostile byte. Every decoder-owned header and payload byte, including vector spare capacity, is volatile-wiped with a compiler fence after successful delivery, every rejection, peer loss, caught unwind and drop. Slice 1 contains no socket, filesystem, process, clock, randomness, wallet, card, camera, display, signing, secret-key, logging or persistence API and makes no target, containment, privilege-enforcement, performance, production or Gate claim. Slice 2 later freezes the calculator's complete twelve-digit, four-function, immediate-execution decimal state machine; CE, red C, Equals, sign, decimal point, overflow, divide-by-zero, every key/state transition and display byte remain deliberately outside slice 1. Two new ring-fenced targets, `qk_ipc_wire` and `qk_ipc_endpoint_state`, each execute exactly 100,000 inputs under pinned `nightly-2026-08-25` with AddressSanitizer, overflow checks and debug assertions, followed by two-copy minimization, hash registration and retained-corpus replay. Their exhaustive named-error oracles cover complete parse/encode identity, truncation, cap claims, fragmentation, coalescing, ancillary presence, direction-kind membership, core-supplied session identity, exact exchange sequencing, one-outstanding behavior, peer-loss terminality, wipe behavior and deterministic repeated outcomes. Existing targets and corpora do not compile or exercise this new leaf and require no requalification. Owner narrowly authorizes this one combined plan-and-slice-1 row at the current six-source-or-test trailing ten-commit window; no slice-2 row may land until at least seven source-or-test commits follow this row. Exact slice-1 scope is only this row in `docs/DECISION-LOG.md`; `host/Cargo.toml`, `host/Cargo.lock`; new `host/qk-ipc/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/wire.rs`, `src/stream.rs`, `src/session.rs`, `src/wipe.rs`, `tests/wire.rs`, `tests/stream.rs`, `tests/session.rs`, `tests/cleanup.rs`, and `tests/surface.rs`; `fuzz/Cargo.toml`, `fuzz/Cargo.lock`, `fuzz/DEPENDENCY-ALLOWLIST.tsv`, `fuzz/README.md`, `fuzz/run-bounded.sh`, `fuzz/replay-corpus.sh`, `fuzz/reproduce.sh`, `fuzz/minimize-corpus.sh`, `fuzz/minimize-finding.sh`, new `fuzz/fuzz_targets/qk_ipc_wire.rs`, `fuzz/fuzz_targets/qk_ipc_endpoint_state.rs`, new `fuzz/corpus/qk_ipc_wire/`, `fuzz/corpus/qk_ipc_endpoint_state/`, new `fuzz/CORPUS-MANIFEST-PROCESS-S1.tsv`, new `fuzz/CAMPAIGN-020.md`, and matching process-slice-1 registration logic in `tools/check-fuzz-corpora.sh`. Every file not named remains byte-frozen, especially `qk-host-sim`, architecture, requirements, resource budgets, open decisions, existing fixtures, targets, corpora, manifests, campaigns, update code and `tools/check.sh`; no other documentation commit is authorized in slice 1. | A fixed hostile-stream leaf and exact one-exchange state contract establish the only byte boundary later product processes may share, while preserving the simulator as a comparison oracle and leaving actual process, socket, device and target enforcement to their dependency-ordered slices. |

| 2026-08-31 | QK-DEC-142 | Owner ratifies process-architecture slice 2 under QK-DEC-140 as a HOST-only minimal `qk-supervisor` lifecycle and mock-device-grant reference plus the dependency-free bounded `qk-decoy` calculator; every other process slice, both QK-DEC-134 hardening lanes, the firmware-ceremony lane and every physical-trigger lane remain closed. Slice 2 introduces no QKIP byte, direction, kind, payload-body grammar, session field or alternative transport: the QK-DEC-140 32-byte header, single-frame stream discipline and all parser precedence remain byte-frozen. `qk-core` still alone mints and validates each 16-byte session id; generation source and non-reuse are explicitly deferred to process slice 4, and slice 2 exposes no live-session-id source, randomness, identifier state or API. The supervisor is a no-secret state owner only: it owns child lifecycle decisions and mock grants, never wallet, card, key, share, transaction or other secret material. The exact lifecycle is `DecoyRunning`, `DecoyStopRequested`, `DecoyReaped`, `RuntimePrepared`, `ProductGrantsInstalled`, `ProductChildrenStarted`, `SessionActive`, `Terminating`, `Terminated`. Initial decoy grants are exactly Display plus Keypad; after the decoy has been reaped, qk-core grants are exactly Display, Keypad and CardSlot and qk-io grants are exactly Camera and RemovableMedia, with no overlapping owner. An explicit supervisor-only wallet request emits `StopDecoy`; only `DecoyReaped` after that request revokes decoy grants and permits `PrepareRuntime`; only `RuntimePrepared` permits `InstallProductGrants`; only confirmed grants permit `StartCoreAndIo`; only confirmed child start permits `EstablishConnection`; and only confirmed connection enters `SessionActive`. Normal closure emits `TerminateProductChildren`, then confirmed reap emits `RemoveRuntime`, and confirmed removal reaches the absorbing `Terminated` state. Any unexpected Decoy, Core or Io loss, connection loss, mock-grant failure, start, stop, reap, runtime or cleanup failure, or invalid transition revokes every grant, emits fail-closed termination and latches `Terminated`; there is no restart, replacement child, retry, fallback or transparent session continuation. Closed supervisor rejections are `InvalidTransition`, `DecoyNotReaped`, `GrantConflict`, `ChildLost`, `ConnectionLost`, `StepFailed`, `CleanupFailed` and `SessionTerminated`, and their displays contain no external byte. Actual process spawning, child binaries, namespace enforcement, real device grants, private-runtime-directory creation, listener acceptance and the complete multi-process harness remain slice-8 work; this slice proves the typed lifecycle and mock boundary only. The slice-1 operating-system deferral is discharged narrowly by a qk-supervisor Unix adapter for a caller-owned connected Unix-domain `SOCK_STREAM`: one `recvmsg` call receives into one caller-owned bounded scratch span and an aligned control span, reports every returned control message or `MSG_CTRUNC` as ancillary presence to `StreamDecoder::ingest` before byte zero is interpreted, closes every delivered `SCM_RIGHTS` descriptor before return, exposes no descriptor, maps zero-byte receive through `StreamDecoder::finish`, rejects every unexpected receive flag as `UnexpectedReceiveFlags`, and reports any syscall failure including interruption as `ReceiveFailed` without exposing errno text. Its named boundary errors are exactly `ScratchEmpty`, `ReceiveFailed`, `UnexpectedReceiveFlags` and the wrapped closed `Ipc` category; one call performs no hidden retry. It returns exact received and consumed byte counts so fragmentation and first-frame-only coalescing retain QK-DEC-140 semantics; it wipes every adapter-consumed scratch byte and every control-buffer byte before return, while any unconsumed coalesced suffix remains caller-owned for ordered resubmission. This narrow unsafe FFI module supports only Linux and Darwin layouts and exists solely because stable Rust lacks the required ancillary-presence receive API; no send path or new wire protocol exists. The exact qk-decoy logical keys are P0.1 `Zero`, `One`, `TwoDown`, `Three`, `FourLeft`, `Five`, `SixRight`, `Seven`, `EightUp`, `Nine`, `Decimal`, `Plus`, `Minus`, `Multiply`, `Divide`, `Percent`, `CeDelete`, `CancelBack` and `EqualsConfirmEnter`; directional markings have no navigation meaning. Calculator phases are exactly `Entry`, `AwaitingRight`, `RightEntry`, `Result`, `Overflow` and `DivideByZero`. Initial and cleared display bytes are ASCII `0`; fault displays are exactly ASCII `OVERFLOW` and `DIVIDE BY 0`. Numeric state is checked signed fixed decimal with no binary floating point; normal display uses fixed notation only, strips fractional trailing zeroes, normalizes negative zero to `0`, and permits at most twelve digit glyphs, with a decimal point and computed minus sign excluded from that count and a leading zero before a fraction included. Entry canonicalizes redundant leading integer zeroes; the first Decimal produces or appends one point, a second is the state-preserving named rejection `DecimalAlreadyPresent`, and a digit beyond the twelve-glyph cap is the state-preserving named rejection `DigitLimitReached`. Minus is always binary subtraction rather than a sign-toggle key; negative literals are not entered, while computed negative results retain one leading minus and may become the next left operand. Arithmetic is immediate and strictly left-to-right with binary Plus, Minus, Multiply and Divide only: a new operator after a complete right entry first evaluates the pending operation and then becomes pending, an operator before any right entry replaces the pending operator, an operator after Result carries that result as the new left operand, and `2 + 3 × 4 =` is `20`. Equals evaluates exactly one complete pending operation; in Entry it finalizes the entry as Result, with a pending operator but no right entry it is `OperandMissing`, and in Result it is `ResultAlreadyFinal`; no repeated-equals history exists. A digit or Decimal after Result starts a new calculation. CE in Entry resets that entry to `0`, in RightEntry discards only the right entry while retaining its left operand and operator, in AwaitingRight is `OperandMissing`, and in Result or either fault resets the complete calculation to `0`; red C resets every phase to initial `0`. Percent is a context-free unary division of the displayed value by 100, never a fifth binary operation: in Entry or Result it produces a Result, in RightEntry it first divides that right value by 100 and then evaluates the pending binary operation, and in AwaitingRight it is `OperandMissing`. In either fault phase, every key except CE and red C is the state-preserving `FaultLatched`. Exact arithmetic is represented as a checked signed numerator over a positive power-of-ten denominator; after every operation, candidate scales are tried from 11 down to zero, each candidate is rounded to nearest with exact ties away from zero, fractional trailing zeroes are removed, and the first fixed rendering with at most twelve digit glyphs is selected. A checked-arithmetic failure or absence of any fitting rendering enters Overflow; an exact zero divisor enters DivideByZero. Closed state-preserving calculator rejections are `DigitLimitReached`, `DecimalAlreadyPresent`, `OperandMissing`, `ResultAlreadyFinal` and `FaultLatched`; no key is silently ignored. There is no memory, history, operator precedence, exponent, scientific mode, wallet-entry path, hidden gesture, layout, font, device input or presentation claim. Headless tests lock all nineteen keys, every key-by-phase cell, exact display bytes, digit and decimal limits, leading zero, sign, percent, CE/red-C, immediate-order, rounding, overflow, divide-by-zero, fault-latch and terminal supervisor behavior. `qk-decoy` has no dependency. `qk-supervisor` depends only on `qk-ipc`. The fail-closed closure guard proves the standalone qk-decoy closure is exactly qk-decoy and therefore excludes qk-ipc and every wallet, card and cryptographic crate; it separately proves the supervisor fuzz closure contains exactly the allowed qk-supervisor/qk-ipc leaf closure, neither new crate enters any legacy default or IPC-only fuzz closure, and the union remains pinned by `fuzz/DEPENDENCY-ALLOWLIST.tsv`. New optional non-default `process-s2-decoy` and `process-s2-supervisor` features gate exactly `qk_decoy_calculator` and `qk_supervisor_lifecycle`. Each target runs exactly 100,000 inputs under pinned `nightly-2026-08-25` with AddressSanitizer, overflow checks and debug assertions, followed by two-copy minimization agreement and hash registration; both use separately written total transition models, named outcomes, repeat consistency, no panic, no silent key, no privilege-before-reap and terminal absorption oracles. Real `recvmsg` plus `SCM_RIGHTS`, fragmentation, coalescing and EOF tests are deterministic integration tests rather than fuzzer syscalls. Body length is checked against the per-kind cap before allocation; fragmentation retains only within that cap, coalescing yields one complete frame at a time, EOF with retained bytes rejects, and a complete frame followed by bytes is not treated as one frame with trailing data. Parser precedence is terminal state, 16-byte availability, magic, version, capability range, descriptor capability, kind range and capability-kind relation, reserved zero, nonzero sequence, per-kind body cap, body availability, stream sequence and outstanding state, then body grammar. Closed QKDV names are `DecoderTerminated`, `HeaderTruncated`, `MagicMismatch`, `VersionMismatch`, `CapabilityOutOfRange`, `CapabilityMismatch`, `KindOutOfRange`, `CapabilityKindMismatch`, `ReservedNonZero`, `SequenceZero`, `SequenceReplay`, `SequenceRegression`, `SequenceSkipped`, `SequenceExhausted`, `OutstandingExchange`, `NoOutstandingExchange`, `ResponseSequenceMismatch`, `ResponseKindMismatch`, `BodyLengthExceeded`, `BodyTruncated`, `TrailingByte`, `UnexpectedFrame`, `ConnectionClosedMidFrame`, `PeerLost`, `OutputBufferTooSmall` and `AllocationFailed`. All decoder and controller allocations, spare capacity included, use the established volatile-wipe ownership on delivery, rejection, caught unwind and drop. Display kinds are `01 Stage`, `02 Profile`, `03 Review`, `04 Result`. Result is profile, route, presence bitmap, each present artifact fact in FinalizedPsbt then RawTransaction order, each present SD receipt in that order, txid[32], wtxid[32]; artifact fact is kind, serialized_len[u32LE], sha256[32], receipt is kind, total_len[u32LE], route 01 is SD and 02 BBQr, and only bitmaps `0f` Simple or Inheritance SD, `01` Simple or Inheritance BBQr, `0a` Quantum Shelter SD and `02` Quantum Shelter BBQr are valid.
